Hello, Doctor. It's, um, it's very nice to meet you. Thank you so much for taking the time to see me. I hope I'm in the right place for this.

Well, I... I suppose it's been about two months now. Just the last two months, really. That's when I became certain that... well, that people on the project were tracking my revisions and, um, planting those errors.

I just want to make sure I'm looking at this the right way, and I'd really value your opinion on it.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that this is happening?\n\nPatient: Well... I really try to be open-minded about it. I don't want to be stubborn or difficult, you know? So when my wife or the junior analysts tell me it's just normal version-control mistakes, I always listen to them. I really, really want to believe them, because... well, it would be much easier if I was just making a fuss over nothing, wouldn't it? \n\nBut... I've been working with these environmental datasets for a long time. And I'm... I'm pretty certain. I mean, despite what they say, I'm almost positive someone is going in and planting those errors on purpose. The way it's being done, it just feels so calculated to make it look like I can't lead the work anymore. \n\nI really don't want to sound unreasonable or paranoid, Doctor. I know how that must sound to you. Do you think I'm overreacting by feeling this sure about it? I just want to be a good professional, but it's so hard to ignore when I can see it happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any episodes of feeling unusually depressed or unusually energized or euphoric around the time this started?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't believe so. I mean, I hope I'm answering that right, but... I haven't felt depressed. And definitely not euphoric or, you know, bouncing off the walls or anything like that. \n\nI really try to keep a very even keel. I don't want to be a burden on my wife or make things harder at the office by being moody, so I just... I just try to stay steady, you know? Just keep my head down and do my work. So my mood has been pretty normal, all things considered. Does that... does that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent head injury, seizure, or new neurological problem?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. Nothing like that at all. I haven't hit my head, and I certainly haven't had any seizures or... or anything neurological. \n\nI've always been very healthy, physically speaking. I make sure to take good care of myself so I don't cause any worry for my family. I haven't even had a headache recently, to be honest. I hope that's the kind of answer you're looking for? I just want to be sure I'm giving you all the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing or seeing things that other people don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, nothing like that. I mean... my eyes and ears are working just fine, thankfully. I haven't been seeing anything out of the ordinary, or hearing voices or... or anything of that nature. \n\nI know sometimes people can imagine things when they're under a lot of stress, but I really try to keep a firm grip on reality. I'm just looking at the numbers and the datasets, exactly as they are on the screen. I hope that's a good sign? I really want to be as helpful as possible, Doctor, so please let me know if I'm giving you the answers you need. I promise I'm not trying to hide anything from you, I just... I haven't experienced anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Aside from this concern about the dataset errors, have you been able to keep up with your work and daily life as usual?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I try my absolute best, I really do. I don't want to let anyone down, especially my team. But, um... to be completely honest, and I hope you won't think too poorly of me for this, but I... I have fallen a bit behind on the modeling deliverables. Just a little bit. It got to the point where the firm had to reassign some pieces of the contract, which... well, it's not ideal. I feel like I haven't been very useful to them lately. \n\nAnd at home... well, I mostly just keep to myself these days. My wife, bless her heart, she's been so patient, but she's had to kind of push me just to keep up with the basic daily routines. I really hope I'm not sounding like a complete failure here, Doctor. I'm trying to get back on track, I promise. Does that... is that the kind of information you needed to know?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people you believe are targeting you have any extraordinary or implausible power or ability, like controlling your thoughts or causing events from a distance?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely nothing like that. I mean, they're just... they're just people, you know? Clever people who know how to manipulate data and ruin a model, but they don't have any sort of... magical powers or anything. \n\nI certainly don't believe anyone is controlling my thoughts or, or doing anything impossible from far away. I really, really want to believe them, because... well, it would be much easier if I was just making a fuss over nothing, wouldn't it? \n\nBut... I've been working with these environmental datasets for a long time. And I'm... I'm pretty certain. I mean, despite what they say, I'm almost positive someone is going in and planting those errors on purpose. The way it's being done, it just feels so calculated to make it look like I can't lead the work anymore. \n\nI really don't want to sound unreasonable or paranoid, Doctor. I know how that must sound to you. Do you think I'm overreacting by feeling this sure about it? I just want to be a good professional, but it's so hard to ignore when I can see it happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any episodes of feeling unusually depressed or unusually energized or euphoric around the time this started?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't believe so. I mean, I hope I'm answering that right, but... I haven't felt depressed. And definitely not euphoric or, you know, bouncing off the walls or anything like that. \n\nI really try to keep a very even keel. I don't want to be a burden on my wife or make things harder at the office by being moody, so I just... I just try to stay steady, you know? Just keep my head down and do my work. So my mood has been pretty normal, all things considered. Does that... does that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent head injury, seizure, or new neurological problem?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. Nothing like that at all. I haven't hit my head, and I certainly haven't had any seizures or... or anything neurological. \n\nI've always been very healthy, physically speaking. I make sure to take good care of myself so I don't cause any worry for my family. I haven't even had a headache recently, to be honest. I hope that's the kind of answer you're looking for? No, nothing like that. I mean... my eyes and ears are working just fine, thankfully. I haven't been seeing anything out of the ordinary, or hearing voices or... or anything of that nature. \n\nI know sometimes people can imagine things when they're under a lot of stress, but I really try to keep a firm grip on reality. I'm just looking at the numbers and the datasets, exactly as they are on the screen. I hope that's a good sign? I really want to be as helpful as possible, Doctor, so please let me know if I'm giving you the answers you need. I promise I'm not trying to hide anything from you, I just... I haven't experienced anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Aside from this concern about the dataset errors, have you been able to keep up with your work and daily life as usual?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I try my absolute best, I really do. I don't want to let anyone down, especially my team. But, um... to be completely honest, and I hope you won't think too poorly of me for this, but I... Does that make sense, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been convinced that the data are being planted?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose it's been about two months now. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I... I wouldn't say I'm completely flat, Doctor. I still care about things, I really do. It's just... maybe I've been a bit more quiet lately? 

I used to love going out, you know. I'd go hiking, or I'd take my telescope out for the local astronomy nights. I really enjoyed mentoring the younger analysts at the firm, too. But lately... I don't know, I just haven't felt the same pull to do those things. 

Sometimes I'll just sit at my desk for hours, or I'll go out to the garage and just sit there with the telescope still covered up. It's not that I'm sad, exactly. I just... I don't really feel the urge to start a project or talk to anyone. I suppose I'm just conserving my energy, really. 

I hope that doesn't make me sound cold or uncaring. I still value my hobbies and my colleagues, I just... I need a bit more downtime right now. Is that fairly normal, Doctor? To just need a break from being so expressive all the time? I hope I'm not giving you the wrong impression.
